## 1. Universal Bounds On Entropy Production From Fluctuating Coarse-grained Trajectories

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Nat. Rev. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Nature Reviews Physics
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-07-20
- **Authors**: Udo Seifert
- **Link**: [https://www.nature.com/articles/s42254-026-00954-5](https://www.nature.com/articles/s42254-026-00954-5)
- **Tags**: `review`  `entropy production`  `stochastic thermodynamics`  `coarse-grained trajectories`  `fluctuation theorems`

### TL;DR

This review presents universal bounds on entropy production derived from coarse-grained trajectories in stochastic thermodynamics, offering model-free estimation methods for non-equilibrium systems.

### Motivation

Entropy production is a key measure of non-equilibrium behavior but is difficult to measure in fluctuating, partially observed systems. The review aims to provide practical methods to infer lower bounds from coarse-grained observables.

### Method

The paper reviews stochastic thermodynamics approaches that use coarse-grained observables such as states, currents, correlations, and waiting times to derive model-free lower bounds on entropy production, applicable to both steady and time-dependent regimes.

### Result

It highlights progress in trajectory-level estimation of entropy production, presenting universal bounds that do not require detailed knowledge of the underlying dynamics.

### Conclusion

The review consolidates recent advances in estimating entropy production from coarse-grained data, providing a valuable resource for experimentalists and theorists in non-equilibrium thermodynamics.

*Keywords: entropy production, stochastic thermodynamics, coarse-grained trajectories, fluctuation theorems, non-equilibrium*

This work advances the theory of multipartite quantum nonlocality and offers practical tools for certifying quantum correlations.

## 6. Bottlenecks In Quantum Channels And Finite Temperature Phases Of Matter

- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Lett.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Letters
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-07-28
- **Authors**: Tibor Rakovszky, Benedikt Placke, Nikolas P. Breuckmann, And Vedika Khemani
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/y9z2-52rl](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/y9z2-52rl)
- **Tags**: `bottleneck theorem`  `Markovian quantum channels`  `steady state`  `finite-temperature phases`  `quantum dynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper proves a bottleneck theorem for Markovian quantum channels, showing that low steady-state weight regions in Hilbert space act as bottlenecks for state transfer, with implications for finite-temperature phases of matter.

### Motivation

The bottleneck theorem is a fundamental result for classical Markov chains, and extending it to quantum channels provides insights into the dynamics and equilibration of open quantum systems, particularly in the context of many-body physics and finite-temperature phases.

### Method

The authors prove an analog of the bottleneck theorem for Markovian quantum channels, using spectral properties and the structure of the steady state to establish that transitions between regions with low steady-state weight are suppressed.

### Result

They demonstrate that if two subspaces are separated by a region with very low weight in the steady state, then the channel's dynamics are effectively constrained, leading to slow relaxation and potential dynamical bottlenecks.

### Conclusion

The result connects quantum information theory (channel bottlenecks) with condensed matter physics (finite-temperature phases), providing a new tool for understanding slow dynamics and metastability in open quantum systems.

*Keywords: bottleneck theorem, Markovian quantum channels, steady state, finite-temperature phases, quantum dynamics*

- **Tags**: `anticoncentration`  `2-design`  `random quantum circuits`  `quantum information`  `quantum supremacy`

### TL;DR

This paper shows that anticoncentration of random quantum circuits is almost sufficient for the 2-design property, overturning a long-held belief and providing new insights into the relationship between these two concepts.

### Motivation

The authors aim to resolve the question of whether anticoncentration implies the 2-design property, which was previously thought to be false. This has implications for quantum information processing, particularly in randomized benchmarking and quantum supremacy.

### Method

The authors provide a rigorous proof that anticoncentration, under certain conditions, implies the 2-design property, and they also construct counterexamples to show the limits of this implication.

### Result

They prove that anticoncentration is almost sufficient for the 2-design property, with a precise characterization of the conditions under which this holds, and they demonstrate that logarithmic-depth circuits anticoncentrate but do not form 2-designs.

### Conclusion

The paper clarifies the relationship between anticoncentration and 2-designs, showing that they are more closely related than previously thought, which has implications for the design of random quantum circuits in various applications.

*Keywords: anticoncentration, 2-design, random quantum circuits, quantum information, quantum supremacy*

### TL;DR

This paper provides a complete theory of the Clifford commutant, offering explicit bases and a graphical calculus, with applications to magic measures and stabilizer property testing.

### Motivation

The Clifford group is central to quantum information, but the structure of its commutant was only partially understood for small tensor powers and qubit numbers. A complete characterization is needed for many applications.

### Method

The authors derive an explicit orthogonal basis for the Clifford commutant for arbitrary tensor power and qubit number, introduce an alternative basis of isotropic sums of Pauli operators, and develop a graphical calculus for manipulating these elements.

### Result

They compute the dimension of the commutant, show the basis is generated by permutations and at most three other operators, and apply the theory to characterize all measurable magic measures and identify optimal strategies for stabilizer property testing.

### Conclusion

The complete theory of the Clifford commutant enables a wide range of applications in quantum information, including magic state resource theory and property testing, and extends to prime-dimensional qudits.

*Keywords: Clifford group, commutant, stabilizer formalism, magic measures, property testing*

- **Tags**: `correlations`  `Bell inequality`  `real quantum theory`  `complex quantum theory`  `quantum networks`

### TL;DR

This paper demonstrates that the gap between real and complex quantum theory in a star network grows linearly with the number of parties, making it arbitrarily large.

### Motivation

Recent work showed that complex quantum theory can produce correlations in certain networks that real quantum theory cannot, but the gap was small. This paper aims to show that the gap can be made arbitrarily large, highlighting the fundamental importance of complex numbers in quantum theory.

### Method

The authors construct a multipartite Bell inequality for a star network with n parties and prove that the maximum violation achievable with complex quantum theory grows linearly with n, while real quantum theory's violation is bounded by a constant.

### Result

The gap between the maximum violations of the Bell inequality in complex and real quantum theories grows linearly with the number of parties, becoming arbitrarily large in the asymptotic limit. They also compute the tolerance of this gap to experimental errors.

### Conclusion

The results show that real quantum theory becomes increasingly inadequate for describing quantum networks as the number of parties grows, reinforcing the necessity of complex Hilbert spaces in quantum information.

*Keywords: Bell inequality, real quantum theory, complex quantum theory, quantum networks, nonlocality*

- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-07-28-2175/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-07-28-2175/)
- **Tags**: `qubit`  `disorder`  `noise`  `decodability`  `stabilizer codes`

### TL;DR

This paper introduces a partition function framework to estimate logical error rates in stabilizer codes, connecting decoding success to statistical mechanics and demonstrating improved sample efficiency.

### Motivation

Estimating logical error rates in quantum error correction is crucial for assessing code performance, but current methods are computationally expensive and sample inefficient, especially at low noise rates.

### Method

The authors define a ratio of partition functions corresponding to maximum likelihood decoding and an order probability for a probabilistic decoder, mapping to disordered statistical mechanics models. They analyze the toric code under bitflip noise and the color code under various noise models.

### Result

They show that the partition function ratio differs from the order probability, and that estimating decoding probability via these quantities is more sample efficient than counting decoder failures. They also discuss the possibility of a decodability boundary and the role of degeneracy in improving decoding.

### Conclusion

The proposed framework provides a more efficient way to estimate logical error curves and offers insights into decoding strategies, potentially aiding in the design of better quantum error correcting codes.

*Keywords: stabilizer codes, partition function, maximum likelihood decoding, toric code, statistical mechanics mapping*

- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `quantum nonlocality without entanglement`  `state discrimination`  `LOCC`  `minimum-error discrimination`

### TL;DR

This paper shows that quantum nonlocality without entanglement can depend on the discrimination measure, constructing ensembles where LOCC fails for minimum-error but succeeds for unambiguous discrimination.

### Motivation

To understand how the choice of discrimination measure affects the phenomenon of quantum nonlocality without entanglement, which is fundamental to quantum information theory.

### Method

The authors construct explicit ensembles of six linearly independent, equally probable bipartite product states and analyze their optimal discrimination under LOCC for both minimum-error and unambiguous discrimination, extending to multipartite systems with numerical evidence.

### Result

They find a separation: LOCC cannot achieve optimal minimum-error discrimination but can achieve optimal unambiguous discrimination for the constructed ensembles, with similar separation observed numerically in multipartite cases.

### Conclusion

The property of quantum nonlocality without entanglement is measure-dependent, highlighting the importance of specifying the discrimination task when studying such phenomena.

*Keywords: quantum nonlocality without entanglement, state discrimination, LOCC, minimum-error discrimination, unambiguous discrimination*

### Motivation

Many quantum information problems involve operator variables that evolve under differential equations, which are not directly handled by standard NPO. The authors aim to provide a general framework to solve such problems.

### Method

They prove that any NPO problem with differential constraints can be transformed into a standard NPO problem under boundedness conditions, enabling the use of existing SDP hierarchies. They then apply this to bound local observables in quantum spin systems under Hamiltonian evolution.

### TL;DR

This paper introduces Shadow Pauli Flow, a new characterization of robust determinism in measurement-based quantum computing with Pauli measurements, proving it necessary and sufficient and computable in polynomial time.

### Motivation

The existing Pauli Flow condition is not necessary for robust determinism in MBQC with Pauli measurements, and there is a need for a complete characterization that also captures correction strategies and computational depth.

### Method

The authors define Shadow Pauli Flow as an extension of Pauli Flow, and prove that an MBQC is robustly deterministic if and only if its correction strategy is consistent with a Shadow Pauli flow. They also provide a polynomial-time algorithm to compute it.

### Result

They demonstrate that Pauli flow is necessary for robust determinism in a weaker sense, and that Shadow Pauli Flow is both necessary and sufficient for full generality, with polynomial-time computability.

### Conclusion

Shadow Pauli Flow provides a complete and efficient characterization of determinism in MBQCs with Pauli measurements, advancing the theory of measurement-based quantum computing.

*Keywords: Measurement-Based Quantum Computing, Pauli Flow, Determinism, Graph States, Quantum Computation*

### Conclusion

The paper resolves the optimal T-count for state preparation and diagonal unitaries, providing both upper and lower bounds, and highlights applications in parallel synthesis.

### TL;DR

This paper characterizes the minimum number of two-qubit Clifford operations (CZ-complexity) needed to prepare graph states, linking it to graph transformations and rank-width.

### Motivation

Understanding the resource cost of preparing graph states is crucial for quantum information processing, as graph states are key resources for measurement-based quantum computing and quantum error correction.

### Method

The authors define CZ-complexity and prove a combinatorial characterization of graph-state transformations using vertex deletions, local complementations, and edge-complementations. They then relate CZ-complexity to rank-width and provide algorithms for interval and circle graphs.

### Result

They show that for any graph with n vertices and rank-width r, the CZ-complexity is O(n*r) and at least Ω(n*r) for connected graphs, with bounds close to optimal. They also give O(n) and O(n log n) algorithms for interval and circle graphs, respectively.

### Conclusion

The work provides a rigorous framework for quantifying the complexity of graph-state preparation, with implications for optimizing quantum circuits and understanding the power of Clifford operations.

*Keywords: graph states, Clifford circuits, CZ-complexity, rank-width, local complementation*

### TL;DR

This paper optimizes measurement schedules for dynamical color codes under measurement-biased noise, introducing the teraquop volume metric and showing that belief matching can significantly improve performance.

### Motivation

Dynamical stabilizer codes may enable large-scale quantum computation, but their performance depends on the measurement schedule, especially when measurements are noisy. The authors aim to optimize schedules for hardware with noisy readouts.

### Method

They analyze dynamically condensed color codes under various noise biases, using the teraquop volume (product of qubits and measurement rounds) to compare performance. They test repeating measurements and compare decoding with minimum-weight perfect matching versus belief matching.

### Result

Repeating measurements improves performance under measurement-biased noise but offers little benefit for unbiased or X-biased noise. The teraquop volume reveals that differences are mainly due to measurement rounds, not qubit count. Belief matching can turn a worst-performing code into a best-performing one.

### Conclusion

The study provides practical guidance for optimizing dynamical codes with noisy measurements, emphasizing the importance of the teraquop volume and correlated-error decoding.

- **Tags**: `topological`  `photonics`  `anisotropy`  `bound states in the continuum`  `topological charges`

### TL;DR

This paper studies topological charges and bound states in the continuum in anisotropic photonic crystal slabs, focusing on their evolution with optical axis orientation.

### Motivation

The authors aim to understand and control topological degeneracies (Dirac/Weyl points, exceptional points) in photonic systems, which are important for photonics and metamaterials.

### Method

They theoretically analyze the band structure of anisotropic photonic crystal slabs, varying the optical axis orientation to break symmetries and track the evolution of bound states in the continuum (BICs), degenerate states (DS), and circularly polarized states (C points).

### Result

They reveal evolutionary laws for DS and BICs under azimuthal anisotropy breaking, including charge annihilation and generation, and show that breaking polar anisotropy leads to BIC splitting, C point migration, and charge reversal at the Γ point.

### Conclusion

The work introduces a new degree of freedom for modulating band degeneracies and topological properties, potentially inspiring future developments in singularity physics using naturally anisotropic materials.

*Keywords: bound states in the continuum, topological charges, photonic crystal slabs, anisotropic materials, band degeneracies*

- **Authors**: Yingying Chen, Zeyi Liu, Zisheng Gong, Cheng Wang, Wendian Yao, Junze Li, Ti Wang, Weibo Gao And Dehui Li
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae846f](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae846f)
- **Tags**: `transport`  `two-dimensional`  `perovskite`  `2D heterostructures`  `exciton transport`

### TL;DR

This paper demonstrates electrically tunable band alignment transitions in WS2/2D-perovskite heterostructures to control exciton transport and valley polarization.

### Motivation

The authors aim to achieve dynamic control of exciton states in van der Waals heterostructures, enabling novel excitonic functionalities for nanophotonic circuits.

### Method

They fabricate WS2/(iso-BA)2PbI4 heterostructures and apply electric fields to switch between type-II and type-I band alignments, converting between charged interlayer and intralayer excitons.

### Result

They achieve 7 μm ON/OFF control of exciton transport and valley polarization switching with a 16.3 ON/OFF ratio.
